2026-10-09T10:06:22.0359307Z shell: /usr/bin/bash -e {0}
2026-10-09T10:06:22.0359499Z env:
2026-10-09T10:06:22.0359702Z   UV_PYTHON_INSTALL_DIR: /home/runner/work/_temp/uv-python-dir
2026-10-09T10:06:22.0359933Z   UV_CACHE_DIR: /home/runner/work/_temp/setup-uv-cache
2026-10-09T10:06:22.0365199Z   QWEN_COMMIT_MESSAGE: [cluster:aspire2ap] [nodes:2] Move one decode worker to prefill with a large single-decode pool (P3/D1, c72)

Why: every recent ASPIRE and Firmus run is prefill-supply bound, while
decode runs far below the 20 ms ITL allowance.
- ASPIRE c64/D196608 (Actions 37872490282): raw input 57960 tok/s on 8
  prefill GPUs (~7.2k tok/s/GPU), P queue mean ~8 per worker, D running
  ~17 per worker, mean ITL 10.17 ms.
- Firmus c96 (37872430145): P queue ~25 per worker, D running ~16,
  ITL 8.5 ms, TTFT 4.2 s -> SLA rejection.
Good output tok/s is therefore approximately
(#prefill GPUs x per-GPU prefill tok/s) / 20, and 8 prefill GPUs cap the
current layout near 2.9k on H100.

The earlier P3/D1 trial (4ac2cf6, Actions 37092369218, 1863.6 good tok/s)
kept the 131072-token decode pool. Its single decode worker was
admission-capped near 29 requests (sampled prealloc queue 11.5, running
24.9, token usage ~0.9), so it measured the pool cap, not the layout.

Change (runtime only, existing image lineage, no require-build):
- configs/aspire-serving.json: node1 GPUs 0-3 become prefill worker 2
  (port 30102, bootstrap 9002); one TP4/EP1 decode worker on node1 GPUs
  4-7 (port 30200). Prefill TP4/EP1/CUTLASS/symmetric memory/fused
  shuffle/tiled routing, EAGLE3 steps3/topk1/draft4 BF16 draft, FP8 KV,
  FA3, chunk 8192, round-robin prefill routing and decode graph buckets are
  unchanged.
- New decode_max_total_tokens=393216 (ASPIRE decode only; prefill and other
  clusters keep 131072). With the 512-token reservation this admits about
  87 requests of 4000+200 tokens. The c64 run showed 16.41 GB free after the
  196608-token pool and 13.12 GB after graph capture per decode rank; the
  extra 196608 tokens cost about 4.4 GB of FP8 target KV plus ~0.4 GB draft
  KV, leaving roughly 8 GB of runtime headroom.
- Client concurrency 48 -> 72. Expected prefill capacity is about
  3 x 29k = 87k input tok/s (~21.7 req/s). With ~3.4 s minimum residence
  (prefill + transfer + 200 tokens at ~14 ms), c72 sits near prefill
  saturation. A companion commit raises only concurrency to 88.

Open risk this run measures: decode ITL at a running batch of ~60. Archived
H100 decode log samples rise from ~10.4 ms (batch 8-15) to ~13.6 ms
(batch 24-31); the slope at higher batch is unmeasured.

Compare against main c48 (~2853-2865 good tok/s on other node pairs; not a
matched pair). Inspect D running batch, ITL distribution, prealloc/transfer
queues, P queue, retractions and peak memory before any follow-up. Not
adoption evidence on its own; organizer accuracy is unaffected by layout but
remains a separate gate.

Validation: bash -n on all scripts, kernel-tag check, Ruff and the full CPU
pytest suite pass. The argv fixture test derives the third prefill worker
and the single decode worker from the archived P2/D2 argv.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
